## Session 5 — 2026-09-25/26 (WP-CPM, base 19173728)

Session 5 opened the CPM work package (charter §9 / §10 lane CPM). It started 2026-09-25 about 20:40 UTC (container
TZ UTC) and ended 2026-09-26. Mode: AUDIT + PLAN ONLY (charter); the session commits on the harness-designated
branch `claude/busy-noether-5oizoe` and opens ONE draft PR (charter §12). No `src/` change, no version bump, no
rebuild. The session ADR is **ADR-0536**. Source for every figure in this section: the lead's validated facts sheet
(session scratch, vanishes with the container) and the records it names; the durable form of every red is the
committed reproducer in `tests/audit/test_audit_20260923_cpm.py` / `_imp.py`.

**Immediate disclosure (charter §6.6), verbatim; placed above the five earlier lines wherever those appear:**

- **T1 — A0923-CPM-001:** every page that prints the schedule-logic (CPM) project finish (/path, /briefing, /brief, /, /portfolio, /forecast, /mission, /trend, /compare, /margin and their APIs) shows the project-calendar date of the finish offset, not the engine's own finish instant: when an elapsed or 24-hour-calendar task drives the finish into project non-working time the date reads a day EARLY (Hard_File_updated3: 12/11/2026 where MS Project, Acumen and SSI show Sat 2026-12-12), and calendar-day finish movements are short by the same day (+35 d for 36). Working-day figures are unaffected. Mechanism since afb8e729 (#497, v1.0.140, 2026-07-31). Until fixed, read the finish from the /path table's rows or the file's own Finish.
- **T1 — A0923-CPM-002 / 003:** on the Large Test File family, an activity whose MS Project split is recorded on the unassigned-work placeholder booking (CPM-002, e.g. UID 7262: 3 working days early, total float 4 days high) and a predecessor linked finish-to-finish to a leveled task (CPM-003, UID 5314: late finish, total and free float 11 working days off) carry CPM figures that differ from MS Project's stored values. CPM-002 wrong at every decidable commit since afb8e729 (v1.0.140; no good commit exists), in its present form since 163d1942 (v1.0.259, ADR-0491); CPM-003 since 5f34c2a8 (v1.0.245, ADR-0474).
- **T1 (latent — no committed file exercises them) — A0923-CPM-005/006/007/008, A0923-IMP-006:** CPM dates and floats are wrong on an operator file that carries a redundant lag-0 SS/SF link from a milestone into an off-calendar task (CPM-005), a worked-day exception on the project calendar (CPM-006), a project start inside the first working block such as 09:00 (CPM-007), logic on a summary task whose children carry custom WBS codes (CPM-008), or an elapsed link lag such as "2ed" (IMP-006). Check an operator file for these shapes before citing its CPM figures.

#### WP-CPM — plan and its QC-3 attack (session 5, 2026-09-25, base `19173728`)

**Deviation, recorded not hidden.** The corpus rebuild (an instrument, owed by WP0) and the FIRST pass of the
differential census ran before this plan was written down (about 30 minutes, same session). Every assumption
below was then attacked on the pristine tree before any finder was spawned or any finding was verified.

**Scope (charter §10 CPM, paths verified at `19173728`):** `engine/cpm.py` (3,322 lines, incl.
`datetime_to_offset` / `offset_to_datetime`), `model/calendar.py`, `engine/driving_slack.py`, `driving_path.py`,
`path_trace.py`, `float_analysis.py`, `path_counterfactual.py`, `drag.py`, `month_axis.py`; plus the presentation
boundary where a CPM instant becomes a displayed date (the charter's "measure the conversion, not the round trip").

**Probes:** (1) the differential census against MS Project's stored values, per field (Start, Finish, LateStart,
LateFinish, TotalSlack, FreeSlack, Critical) and per file, over the 44-file corpus, every non-exact class explained
or ledgered; the project finish as a field of its own; (2) metamorphic relations (finder F-META); (3) the edge
matrix — links, lags and constraints (finder F-EDGE-L) and calendars, progress and special tasks (finder F-EDGE-C);
(4) the census's non-exact residual classified against the register and the ADRs (finder F-RES).

### The corpus instrument and the differential census (session 5)

- **The 44-file corpus, rebuilt from scratch** (the instrument session 1 left undone): the 15 committed goldens
  (`find tests/fixtures -name "*.mspdi.xml*"`, 11 gzipped) + the 29 tracked intake `.mpp` (all 29 OLE2, magic
  `d0cf11e0a1b11ae1`), converted with `java -cp "tools/mpxj/classes:tools/mpxj/lib/*" MpxjToMspdi <in> <out>` under a
  JVM flock, ONE output per INPUT path, index-prefixed (01..29): 29 of 29 rc=0. **22,105 scheduled activities by two
  methods** — the engine's timings, and a raw-XML ElementTree filter (`Summary≠1, IsNull≠1, Active≠0, UID≠0`). The
  44 files are **24 distinct saves** (F-RES: grouped by an identical stored-field fingerprint).
- **Method.** Stored values are read from the raw MSPDI with ElementTree (independent of the importer); engine values
  from `compute_cpm(parse_mspdi_text(...))`; "rendered" = the wall instant when present, else the axis rendering.
  F-RES re-ran the lead's census script and got a byte-identical output (27,258 lines, 44 files, 27,214 task rows,
  22,105 timed), and an independent tally of its own reproduced every first-pass figure exactly.
- **First-pass figures (lead; re-derived byte-identically by F-RES):**

### Probe families and their reach — the negatives (a negative is a statement about the probe)

- **F-META (metamorphic relations; population the 44 files):** R1 renumber `<ID>` / rename `<Name>` / both — 132
  runs, 0 violations; R2 reorders (PredecessorLink, Exception, WeekDay, sibling leaves, TimephasedData, Resource,
  Calendar) — 0 violations; only `<Assignment>` order moved anything (CPM-004's documented tie-break, not
  nondeterminism: 8 of 16 tied plans change `late_finish_wall` only); R3 transitively redundant FS0 and FF0 links —
  26,301 per type, 0 CPM violations (SS0 / SF0 → CPM-005; the FS0 / FF0 driving-slack case is the F-META R3 lead below);
  R4 whole-week shifts (+7, +364, −364 days) — 132 runs, 0 violations; R5 removal of 49 completed isolated tasks — 0
  changes (0 inactive tasks in the corpus). Records: `finder-F-META/RELATIONS.md`, `NEGATIVES.md` (session scratch).
- **F-EDGE-L (links / lags / constraints):** the corpus carries LagFormat 7 only (34,674 elements in 72 MSPDI), FS /
  SS / FF (no SF), leads on FS only, constraints ASAP / SNET / FNET / MSO / MFO + 25 deadlines (no ALAP / SNLT / FNLT
  in any MS Project save); FS positive-lag successors 35 / 35 agree, FF 3 / 3; 158 non-zero-lag instances checked;
  constraint rows agree to 0 working seconds or within a minute except EVM2 25 / 26 (HELD F2-H1) and
  Hard_File_updated2 UID 189 (66 s); hand-built link type × working lag 17 / 17 agree. Records: `MATRIX.md`,
  `NEGATIVES.md` in `finder-F-EDGE-L` (session scratch).
- **F-EDGE-C (calendars / progress / special tasks):** no timezone-aware arithmetic in engine / model / importers;
  every CPM output byte-identical under TZ=America/New_York and UTC over the 44 files + 4 synthetic schedules crossing
  2026-03-08 / 2026-11-01; elapsed durations — 16 corpus tasks exact; leap day — 51 corpus tasks exact; year-end /
  weekend exceptions exact (synthetic); 24-hour task calendars' misses all in documented classes; Night Shift

## How to read this ledger

- **Where the records live.** Every figure below is copied from a session record: `audit/LEAD-DECISIONS.md`
  (the lead's validation record — authoritative where records disagree), `audit/ID-MAP.json`, the finder
  records (waves 0–1), the verifier verdicts (wave 2), the teeth and exposure records (wave 3), the lead's
  second-verification tables, and the gate outputs. `$S` abbreviates the session scratchpad
  (`/tmp/claude-0/-home-claude/a499c922-7df2-5cb5-a61f-b1862e5269c9/scratchpad`). **Scratch vanishes with the
  container**: the red commands are quoted exactly as they were run (so a reader can see what was measured),
  but the durable, re-runnable form of every red is the finding's reproducer, which builds its inputs inline
  or from committed fixtures at the stated sha. This is a known departure from the charter's "never a pointer
  to scratch" working rule (§3) and is recorded here rather than hidden.
- **Quoting.** Long records are clipped with "…"; a clip never splices two quotes together. A field with no
  record reads **not recorded**. Anything one party has not confirmed reads **UNVERIFIED**.
- **Blocks.** One block per CONFIRMED-DEFERRED class (47 in session 1; after session 2: 46 retained blocks — 45 CONFIRMED-DEFERRED + 1 FIXED-UPSTREAM — and TST-003's block moved under Non-confirmed records; session 5: 56 retained blocks — 55 CONFIRMED-DEFERRED + 1 FIXED-UPSTREAM — with lane CPM and IMP-006 / IMP-007 added). "Verification 1" is the independent verifier
  (fresh context, claim-only packet, charter §6.2); "Verification 2" is the lead's own re-run (§6.3).
  "Teeth" is the `prove-able-to-fail` three-part proof run by the wave-3 assemblers in clones of the base
  (§6.5). Fix sketches, moving pins and exposure windows appear for T1, T2 and LAW-1 only (§6.6).
- **Counting.** Classes, not instances (§5): each block carries its measured instance count and census method.
- **Randomized probes.** No finder, verifier or reproducer record in this session uses a random seed; every
  block's seed field therefore reads "none". (Session 5: no finding rests on a randomized probe, so every session-5 block's seed also reads "none"; finder F-EDGE-C's record lists one seeded crash / hang fuzz — seeds 20260925 and 7, 1,200 schedules, 0 crash — see the Session 5 section.)
- **Falsification pass (session 2).** Every retained block ends with the bullet "Falsification pass (session 2, refuter packet Rnn)": the verdict, the eight attacks A1–A8 quoted verbatim from the refuter's JSON (clipped with "…"; a [bracketed] word is drafter glue), the refuter's tier opinion, what would have refuted it, and — for DOC-004, IMP-002 and DOC-014 — the narrowing or the upstream fix and the matching test edit. A7 is the refuter's own re-run on f1b691f3.

## Status legend — read this before trusting a row

Definitions from the charter (§4, §5), plus the two session-2 words at the end; the ledger uses no other status words.

| status | meaning |
| --- | --- |
| **CANDIDATE** | a finder's claim with claim, verbatim authority, red, control and class census persisted to disk (§6.1); not yet verified |
| **REPRODUCED** | an independent verifier with fresh context reproduced the claim from scratch at the sha and attempted refutation (§6.2) |
| **CONFIRMED-DEFERRED** | the campaign's terminal state for a real defect: "reproduced, reproducer committed, fix priced in the plan" (§5) — this session: reproducer produced as a file, not committed (MODE); session 4 committed them, and session 5 commits its ten on the campaign branch |
| **UNVERIFIED** | "a verification is missing or incomplete; re-queued" (§5); no verdict is not a verdict |
| **REFUTED** | the claim was probed and did not hold; the probe is cited |
| **NON-REPRODUCED** | an honest probe could not produce the claimed symptom; what would settle it is stated |
| **UNVERIFIABLE** | cannot be settled from this side; the settling observation is named |
| **ARTIFACT-GATED** | needs something only the operator can produce (a fresh Fuse or SSI export, an MS Project run, a Windows observation); at most PLAUSIBLE here; goes to the ASKS file (§4) |
| **DUPLICATE-OF** `<ID>` | the same defect class as an existing row (this or a prior audit), cited by its ID |
| **HELD-BY** `<ADR>` | measured, and deliberately left by a documented decision whose premise still holds (§4 "not errors") |
| **FIXED-UPSTREAM** | (session 2) the defect was reproduced at the base and is gone on the current `origin/main`, closed by a named upstream commit; kept in the register, excluded from the repair plan, its reproducer converted to a passing pin with a measured negative control |
| **WITHDRAWN** | (session 2) the claim was measured true but falls under charter §4 "not errors" (disagreement with a documented deliberate decision whose premise still holds); omitted from the register count, its record kept under Non-confirmed records, its reproducer removed |

This session's tally (LEAD-DECISIONS): **47 CONFIRMED-DEFERRED** classes · **3 HELD-BY** · **2 REFUTED** ·
**1 DUPLICATE-OF R-32** · **1 UNVERIFIED** candidate (UI-001) · no NON-REPRODUCED, UNVERIFIABLE or
ARTIFACT-GATED verdicts (one sub-question inside a REFUTED record — SSI's display of a sub-day negative slack —
stays UNVERIFIED with its settling observation named). The 50 claims sent to verifiers in 12 packets all
returned REPRODUCED, several NARROWED; where a verifier narrowed a claim, its scope wins.

**Session-2 tally (falsification pass, LEAD-DECISIONS-2):** 47 in → **0 REFUTED · 44 NOT-REFUTED · 3 NARROWED · 1 FIXED-UPSTREAM (DOC-014) · 1 WITHDRAWN (TST-003)** → **46 retained, 45 open**; HELD-BY 3, REFUTED 2 and the DUPLICATE (now CLOSED UPSTREAM, verified) unchanged; UI-001 promoted to CANDIDATE; two new UNVERIFIED leads.

## Testimony-risk tiers — quoted verbatim

From `docs/STATE/AUDIT-2026-08-27-REPORT.md` §0, lines 24–31 (read at 8c71c639), unchanged (charter §5:
"Quote that table into the ledger; do not paraphrase it"):

| tier | what a wrong item costs on the stand |
| --- | --- |
| **T1** | a FIGURE the analyst would cite is, or could be, WRONG — the engine, a metric basis, a CPM rule, a parity leg |
| **T2** | the figure is right but could be MISREAD — mislabelled, misattributed, a wrong unit, an unstated basis, a defaulted value that looks measured |
| **T3** | the RECORD — Law 1's transaction log, what the tool discloses about itself, the documents that describe it |
| **T4** | CONTROLS and RENDERING that could hide evidence or mislead a reader — a dead control, a race, an overflow, a stale header |
| **T5** | PROCESS — CI, instruments, tests that could not fail, figures of unknown provenance |
| **T6** | ORGANIZATIONAL — decisions that are not engineering's to make |

Law 1 bypasses additionally carry the flag **LAW-1** and are disclosed like a T1 (charter §5, §6.6).

## Findings — one block per class

Each block: claim (the verifier's narrowed wording where it narrowed) · authority · basis · red · control ·
class census · seed · verification 1 (independent verifier) · verification 2 (lead) · lead validation ·
reproducer · teeth; T1 / T2 / LAW-1 blocks add the fix sketch, the pins that move and the exposure window;
every block ends with its repair unit (LEAD-DECISIONS U01–U21) and, since session 2, its falsification bullet.

### A0923-AI-001 · AI · T1 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, an Ask answer that spells an unsourced count out ('Thirteen activities are behind baseline.', 'Forty …', 'Ninety percent …') via POST /api/ask/{name} or POST /api/ask is ACCEPTED in strict mode and carries no annotate footer, while the digit form is discarded ('13', '17') or at least flagged ('40', '90' pass strict only with a derived-figure note), because ai/qa.py's _classify_figures/_figure_roles iterate _TOKEN_RE over raw text instead of citations.figure_tokens(); ADR-0239:23-28 requires an introduced spelled-out count to fail and says strict/annotate Q&A inherit that fix.

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: for '40'/'90' the digit form is not discarded in strict but accepted with a '[Derived figures …]' note, so there the contrast is flagged vs unflagged.

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0239-ai-figure-gate-hardening.md:23-28: '2. **M4:** `figure_tokens` gains a bounded number-word lexicon (two…ninety + scale words + zero + dozen), each tokenizing to its digit string — "twelve" and "12" are the SAME evidence token, so a legitimate rephrase passes and an introduced spelled-out count fails. ... One tokenizer feeds every gate, so strict/annotate Q&A and the dual-model cross-check inherit the fix.' (read at 8c71c639) (+1 further authority citation(s) in the finder record)

### A0923-AI-002 · AI · T1 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, a sign flip written with U+2212, U+2013 or seven other dash-like code points passes every figure gate — strict and annotate Ask ('−134' ACCEPTED with no footer where ASCII '-134' is DISCARDED against the engine's '+134'), the dual-model cross-check, the Executive Briefing polish ('−143 workday(s) … (ahead of plan)') and /api/translate ('+12' → '−12') — and the engine's own text 'DCMA-14' seeds '-14' as a citable value; ADR-0131:58-60 (M6) requires a sign flip to change the figure multiset and fail.

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: broader than claimed (9 non-ASCII dash code points bypass); narrative polish had no signed figure to flip, so there the measured case is a sign introduced on an unsigned figure.

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0131-audit-cluster-remediation-batch1.md:58-60: '7. **M6 — figure-gate is sign-aware.** `ai/citations._FIGURE_RE` gains an optional leading `-`, so a rephrase that flips `-5 days` (ahead) to `5 days behind` changes the multiset and forces the verbatim fallback. Sign is load-bearing in schedule forensics (variance / float / slip direction).' (read at 8c71c639) (+2 further authority citation(s) in the finder record)
- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE — A2 (the repo's own contract, independent of the code under test): ADR-0131:58-60, CLAUDE.md:230-231, ADR-0239:19-22; +134 re-derived by date arithmetic.
- **Red** (as run; `$S` = session scratch):

  ```text
  $ python3 $S/audit/wave-1/F3-AI/harness/probe_b5c_ask_workbook_signflip.py
  $ python3 $S/audit/wave-1/F3-AI/harness/probe_b5_signflip_routes.py
  $ python3 $S/audit/wave-1/F3-AI/harness/probe_b5d_hyphen_seeding.py (each prints to stdout; saved copies: the matching *.out.txt in this directory)
  ENGINE FACT IN THE PROMPT: ... Project2.mspdi.xml 2027-09-14; Project5.mspdi.xml 2028-01-26. From Project2.mspdi.xml to Project5.mspdi.xml that date …
  [strict  ] [CONTROL ascii '-'] model: 'The computed finish moved -134 calendar days across the series.' -> DISCARDED
  [strict  ] [U+2212          ] model: 'The computed finish moved −134 calendar days across the series.' -> ACCEPTED, no footer
  [annotate] [U+2212          ] ... -> ACCEPTED, no footer
  /api/ai/briefing [U+2212 minus    ] polished=True headline: In one sentence: Project5.mspdi.xml is forecast to finish on Wednesday, January 26, 2028 …
  /api/translate   [CONTROL ascii '-'] -> (source kept — translation discarded)
  … (2 more lines in the record)
  ```

### A0923-CUI-002 · CUI · T3 · LAW-1 (transport; no schedule content measured) · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, ai/ollama_process._DIRECT_OPENER (ProxyHandler({}) without _NoRedirect) follows a 3xx from whatever answers on the loopback Ollama port to another host — GET /api/ps on 301/302/303/307/308, the unload POST as a body-less GET on 301/302/303 — and counts the off-box follow as a successful unload; ADR-0070:25-31 decides the local-AI opener refuses redirects, and ADR-0469:27 lists ai/ollama_process.py as '(loopback)'.

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: no schedule content was carried in any measured follow (only the connection, the redirect-chosen URL, Host and User-Agent); the trigger needs a local peer on the Ollama port answering 3xx; on the real shutdown path the redirected drain probe let the session declare a clean exit.

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0469-wp7-the-transaction-log-under-law-1-and-the-twenty-one-never-adverse-routes-driven-adversely.md:27: 'exactly six: `ai/gateway.py` (the recorded remote path), `ai/ollama.py`, `ai/ollama_process.py` (loopback), `launcher.py` (the loopback handshake), `web/system.py`, `importers/mpp_mpxj.py` (local binaries) | **PINNED** — a seventh is a Law-1 event by name' (read at 8c71c639) (+1 further authority citation(s) in the finder record)
- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE — A2 (ADR-0070:25-31 decision; ADR-0469:27 inventory); settled with the real opener against real loopback sockets and a simulated remote (no real egress).

  - A7 current main (f1b691f3): STILL-PRESENT on f1b691f3: PYTHONPATH=main-now/src (schedule_forensics.__file__ under main-now/src); 302: _loaded_models returned [] with '192.0.2.10 received: [GET /collect Host=192.0.2.10]'; unload POST followed as a body-less GET, unloaded=1; shutdown declared clean (engaged=False, marker cleared) on the redirected drain probe. git diff 8c71c639..f1b691f3 -- ai/ollama_process.py launcher.py: no change.
  - A8 steelman, and why it fails: Steelman: (1) the cleanup requests carry no schedule content — a body-less GET with Host and User-Agent is not CUI … (2) the trigger needs a hostile process already bound to the loopback Ollama port on the operator's own machine … Assessment: (1) and (2) are true and are the verifier's narrowing — this is a transport/beacon defect …, not a content leak; that is why the LAW-1 flag is 'transport'. (3) does not cover the measured shutdown outcome: the DRAIN re-probe … also followed the redirect and accepted the off-box peer's empty list … a fix is one line (reuse ollama._make_opener()). The defence narrows severity; it does not make the behaviour intended or the record true.
  - Refuter's tier opinion: T3 (the pinned transport inventory and the module's assurances are false as measured) with a LAW-1 transport flag, low severity: no schedule content leaves; the trigger is a local peer on the Ollama port answering 3xx …
  - What would have refuted it (refuter): A real loopback server answering 302/307 to /api/ps or /api/generate producing an HTTPError (or None) from the cleanup calls with NO connect() to the Location host in strace and unload_loaded_models returning 0; or an ADR/HELD row stating the cleanup and launcher openers deliberately follow redirects.

### A0923-WEB-001 · WEB · T4 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, a POST /upload carrying more than 1,000 file parts is refused by the multipart parser's default limit before the handler runs (1,001 → 400 'Too many files. Maximum number of files is 1000.'; 1,000 → 200), and home.js never checks resp.ok, so the page reloads with no message and nothing loaded — also when a picked folder's non-schedule files push the part count past 1,000; ADR-0225's operator rule is 'no file-count cap' with per-file failures recorded (fail loud), and README.md:69-70 promises 'no silent failures'.

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: the limit counts multipart FILE parts, non-schedule files included (600 schedules + 401 other files fails the same way); the README half is weak alone (it still says 'up to 100 at once') — ADR-0225 and app.py:1981-1983 carry the requirement.

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0225-grouped-ingestion-and-portfolio.md:20-24: '... Operator rules (confirmed): … **no file-count cap**.' and :48-50 'The **100-file cap is removed**; non-schedule files inside a folder are **skipped** (not errored); per-file failures are still recorded and skipped (fail loud).' · README.md:69-70: 'Files parse locally; the dashboard tells you exactly what loaded and what failed (no silent failures).' (as quoted by the verifier, read at 8c71c639) · public: https://www.starlette.io/requests/ 'Signature: request.form(max_files=1000, max_fields=1000, max_part_size=1024*1024)' (retrieved 2026-09-23)

### A0923-WEB-002 · WEB · T4 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, an endpoint string urllib.parse cannot split ('http://[', 'http://a＠127.0.0.1:11434', or the ordinary typos 'http://[::1:11434' and 'http://[127.0.0.1]:11434') makes net_guard.is_local_http_endpoint raise ValueError instead of returning False, so POST /settings and GET /api/ai/models answer 500 and a settings file carrying it makes create_app()/launcher.main() raise (the desktop message blames the virtualenv); ADR-0404:41-42 says 'Missing or corrupt files yield pure defaults — a launch never fails on settings.'

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: holds in full and extends to ordinary typos; the ADR-0423 citation is analogical only; a malformed value enters the file only by hand-edit or external write — the case ADR-0404 names. Sibling: POST /language 500s on a malformed Referer (app.py:7941).

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0404-persistent-ai-settings.md:37-42: '3. **Loading is a trust boundary.** The file is operator-editable state, so loading re-applies the POST sanitizers: ... non-loopback local endpoints fall back to defaults, ... Missing or corrupt files yield pure defaults — a launch never fails on settings.' (read at 8c71c639) (+1 further authority citation(s) in the finder record)

### A0923-IMP-004 · IMP · T2 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, an MSPDI declaring encoding="windows-1252" (or ISO-8859-1) in that encoding is decoded as UTF-8 with errors='replace' by parse_mspdi (importers/mspdi.py:135) and POST /upload (web/app.py:8166), so 'Café façade – Müller’s pour' loads with 5 U+FFFD, import_notes=(), no log line and 'loaded 1 schedule(s); 0 rejected'; W3C XML 1.0 §4.3.3 makes an unprocessable declared encoding a fatal error, never a silent substitution.

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: holds for windows-1252 (5 × U+FFFD) and ISO-8859-1 (3 × U+FFFD); the garbled name is visible on /analysis but unexplained; MPXJ behaves the same by default; no committed file is affected. The UTF-16 sub-claim is REFUTED as silent (it fails loudly as malformed).

- **Authority** — [independent] W3C Extensible Markup Language (XML) 1.0 (Fifth Edition), §4.3.3 'Character Encoding in Entities', https://www.w3.org/TR/xml/ (retrieved 2026-09-23, sentences grepped from the raw HTML): "It is a fatal error when an XML processor encounters an entity with an encoding that it is unable to process." and "All XML processors MUST be able to read entities in both the UTF-8 and UTF-16 encodings." and "it is a fatal error for an entity including an encoding declaration to be presented to the XML processor in an encoding other than that named in the declaration". …
- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE — A1 W3C XML 1.0 §4.3.3 (retrieved 2026-09-23).
- **Red** (as run; `$S` = session scratch):

  ```text
  $ python3 $S/audit/wave-1/F1-IMP/probe_H5.py (documents built inline, written to a TemporaryDirectory; output saved as probe_H5.out.txt)
  [CONTROL utf-8] parse_mspdi -> name='Café façade – Müller’s pour' (U+FFFD x0) import_notes=()
  [windows-1252] parse_mspdi -> name='Caf� fa�ade � M�ller�s pour' (U+FFFD x5) import_notes=() log_records=[only the unrelated CalendarUID warning]
  [ISO-8859-1 …] parse_mspdi -> name='Caf� fa�ade M�ller' (U+FFFD x3) import_notes=()
  [UTF-16 (BOM, LE)] parse_mspdi -> ImporterError: 'malformed MSPDI XML: not well-formed (invalid token): line 1, column 0'
  ```

### A0923-IMP-005 · IMP · T4 · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, an .xlsx whose worksheet row carries r='²' or r='①' (or a mixed '1²') uploaded to POST /onepager/upload or POST /onepager-compare/upload answers a bare HTTP 500, because reports/xlsx_read.py:229 gates int() with str.isdigit() and the ValueError is not the XlsxError the routes catch; ADR-0423 closed exactly this isdigit()-gated int() class ('isdecimal() is the exact predicate').

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: holds in full; ADR-0423 claims closure of the predicate class (not no-500 in general), which this site re-opens. Incidental record note: four code comments cite ADR-0524 for the numbered reader where ADR-0526 is meant.

- **Authority** — docs/adr/0423-isdigit-gated-int-500d-twelve-routes-and-isdecimal-is-the-exact-predicate.md:10-13: '`str.isdigit()` is **True** for superscripts (`²`), circled forms (`①`) and many other Unicode numeric characters that `int()` rejects with `ValueError`. An `isdigit()`-gated conversion is therefore not a guard at all: it admits the value and then raises. Typing a superscript into an ordinary form field answered **500**.' + title '... and `isdecimal()` is the exact predicate' (read at 8c71c639) (+1 further authority citation(s) in the finder record)

### A0923-IMP-006 · IMP · T1 · latent (0 elapsed LagFormats in the 72 committed MSPDI) · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 19173728, a hand-built MSPDI (Standard 08-12/13-17 Mon-Fri calendar, start Mon 2026-06-01 08:00; Z 5d -FS0-> A 5d -FS+2ed-> B 2d, the lag written <LinkLag>28800</LinkLag><LagFormat>8</LagFormat>) via parse_mspdi_text + compute_cpm yields B finishing 2026-06-24 17:00 (the model holds lag_minutes=2880 WORKING minutes = 6 working days; 12 eh and 12 h import to the identical Relationship); Microsoft's documented semantics (LagFormat 8 = elapsed days; elapsed time counts all time including non-working time; LinkLag in tenths of a minute) require B to start Mon 2026-06-15 08:00 and finish Tue 2026-06-16 17:00 (6 working days earlier).

- **Authority** (the claim-only packet's citations, verbatim, clipped; 9 item(s)) — https://learn.microsoft.com/office-project/xml-data-interchange/durationformat-element?view=project-client-2016: "Elapsed time counts all time, including non-working time specified in the project, resource, or task calendar. For example, if the calendar specifies Saturday and Sunday as non-working days, a duration of 7d is seven working days such as Monday – Friday and the following Monday and Tuesday. A duration of … / https://learn.microsoft.com/office-project/xml-data-interchange/linklag-element?view=project-client-2016: "The amount of lag in tenths of a minute. ... LinkLag requires a LagFormat to be specified." / https://learn.microsoft.com/office-project/xml-data-interchange/predecessorlink-element?view=project-client-2016: "LagFormat | Optional | Indicates the time format (hours, days, and so on) for the amount of lag specified in LinkLag." (+6 further citation(s) in the packet)
- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE
- **Red** (the lead's re-run on the pristine tree at `19173728`; `$S` = session scratch, vanishes with the container — the durable form is the reproducer below):

  ```text
  python3 $S/audit/wave-6/finder-F-EDGE-L/red/red_elapsed_lag.py
  ```

*Scope (the verifier's narrowing, adopted):* Verifier: holds for the three named UIDs (3 of the 5 pressure rows); the page explains the two bases elsewhere (the DCMA parity comparison), unattached to either table; the pressure table agrees with MS Project/Acumen — the divergent value is the grid's.

- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE — A2 (the page's own statements analysis.py:657-658, :729; ADR-0220:18-19) + A1 MS Project's stored TotalSlack in the golden.

**Claim.** At 8c71c639, two tracked nested CLAUDE.md files under 00_REFERENCE_INTAKE/ carry directives contrary to the root rules (00_REFERENCE_INTAKE/CLAUDE.md:141 and :192 link Lucide and fonts from a CDN; the design-handoff CLAUDE.md:6 calls itself 'your standing contract'), .claude/settings.json has no claudeMdExcludes, and Anthropic's public documentation says subdirectory CLAUDE.md files 'load on demand when Claude reads files in those directories'; root CLAUDE.md:290-291 requires 'no CDN, no bundler'.

### A0923-CPM-001 · CPM · T1 · in committed corpus · CONFIRMED-DEFERRED

**Claim.** At 19173728, loading tests/fixtures/golden/fuse_hardfile/Hard_File_updated3.mspdi.xml.gz into the app (create_app(SessionState()) + POST /upload) and requesting GET /path, /briefing and /brief displays the schedule-logic (CPM) project finish as 12/11/2026 (Friday December 11, 2026 / 2026-12-11), whereas MS Project's stored project FinishDate (and its latest stored task Finish) is 2026-12-12T17:00:00 and the engine's own CPMResult.project_finish_wall is 2026-12-12 17:00. Same shape on the 24-hour snapshot (Hard_File_updated3_24hr: stored FinishDate 2026-11-19T01:00:00, displayed 2026-11-18).

*Scope (the lead's, from both verifications):* every page that prints the CPM project finish renders `offset_to_datetime(project_finish)` and ignores `CPMResult.project_finish_wall`: Hard_File_updated3 shows 12/11/2026 where MS Project, Acumen Fuse, SSI and the engine's own wall read Sat 2026-12-12 17:00 (24-hour save: 11/18 for 2026-11-19 01:00). Working-day figures (float, the "+26 wd slip") are unaffected — both instants are the same working minute; calendar-date surfaces and calendar-day arithmetic are wrong.

- **Authority** — A1 MS Project's FinishDate (golden line 12, read with ElementTree) = the latest stored task Finish, 2026-12-12T17:00 (UIDs 146 / 411 / 155; UID 146 is 48 elapsed hours, DurationFormat 8, Thu 12-10 17:00 → Sat 12-12 17:00 — Microsoft Learn, DurationFormat element: elapsed time counts non-working time). Witnesses independent of the MPXJ goldens: Acumen Fuse v8.11.0 "Hard_File_updated2 vs update3 Forensic Analysis Report.xlsx", sheet Projects: Hard_File_updated3 Finish 2026-12-12 17:00 (serial 46368.708333), Time Now 2026-10-12 17:00 (the golden's StatusDate), Hard_File_updated2 Finish 2026-11-06 17:00 → a 36-day move; SSI "Hard File updated3_UID_155_Directional_Path_Analysis_2026-7-15.xlsx": UIDs 146, 155, 411 finish Sat 2026-12-12; the 24-hour SSI export: 2026-11-19 01:00. A2 `engine/cpm.py:291-295` (the `project_finish_wall` docstring: "…None when every task follows the project calendar — offset_to_datetime(project_finish) is then exact"); `docs/PARITY-REPORT.md:253` records the file's CPM finish "exact" (2026-12-12) and :254 the 24-hour save's (2026-11-19 01:00) — measured on the wall (`tests/parity/test_hard_file_stored_dates_oracle.py:154-156` pins `project_finish_wall or offset_to_datetime`), i.e. the parity claim is proven on a figure no page prints.
- **Basis** — ENGINE≠ORACLE (the engine's own wall is right; the rendering half is wrong) — MS Project's stored FinishDate, Acumen Fuse's Projects sheet and SSI's directional-path export.
- **Red** (verifier V2's red command, byte-identical to the packet's; the durable form is the reproducer below):

  ```text

  assert c.post('/upload', files={'files': ('Hard_File_updated3.mspdi.xml', xml, 'text/xml')}).status_code == 200
  html = c.get('/path').text
  assert '12/12/2026' in html and '12/11/2026' not in html, 'the /path page shows the CPM finish as 12/11/2026'

Same under TZ=America/New_York (red_output_NY.txt) and under Python 3.13.12 in a scratch venv importing the checkout's src (py313_output.txt). Both halves fail: '12/12/2026' in /path = False; '12/11/2026' in /path = True (control_output.txt). Rendered text: /path '... On the critical path 12/11/2026 Computed finish ...'; /briefing 'schedule logic landing on Friday …
  ```

#### A0923-CPM-009 · CPM · ARTIFACT-GATED (ASK-12) — SNLT / FNLT under HonorConstraints=1

- **Claim (as sent to the verifier).** With `<HonorConstraints>1</HonorConstraints>`, an SNLT / FNLT task that logic
  pushes past its date keeps its LOGIC dates (hand-built MSPDI: B 2026-06-15 08:00..06-16 17:00, C finish 2026-06-17
  17:00, project finish offset 6240) while MSO / MFO siblings are held at their dates (finish offset 4800); the flag is
  never read (HonorConstraints=0 and =1 give byte-identical output).
- **Mechanism reproduced.** Verifier P3 (`A0923-CPM-009.json`): **REPRODUCED** for the mechanism, basis ARTIFACT-GATED;
  lead re-run: "FAIL SNLT 2026-06-10T08:00:00 violated by logic, HonorConstraints=1: B 2026-06-15 08:00..2026-06-16 17:00
  (expected 2026-06-10 08:00..2026-06-11 17:00); C finish 2026-06-17 17:00 (expected 2026-06-12 17:00); B
  total_float=-1440; project_finish offset 6240" (FNLT the same), exit 1; control (`control_snlt_fnlt_precedence.py`,
  five cells — MSO, MFO and three SNLT / FNLT cells) all PASS, exit 0.
- **Why it is gated (verifier, verbatim, clipped).** "That the engine's SNLT/FNLT output differs from MS Project's is
  supported by Microsoft's generic HonorConstraints / PROJ_OPT_HONOR_CONSTRAINTS definitions and by MPXJ's
  MicrosoftScheduler, but NOT pinned: Microsoft's own 'Definition of Microsoft Project constraints' KB defines SNLT as
  'LS=CD(if Date<LS)' and FNLT as 'LF=CD (if Date<LF)' -- late-date-only, i.e. the engine's rule -- and no committed MS
  Project save carries an SNLT/FNLT in conflict." Census (verifier): 72 MSPDI documents — SNLT 0, FNLT 8 (all in two
  repo-authored synthetic files); SNLT / FNLT violated by a dependency under HonorConstraints=1: **0**.
- **Tier opinion (verifier).** "T1 (latent) IF MS Project confirms … Until an MS Project run settles the SNLT/FNLT
  conflict semantics it is at most PLAUSIBLE (charter §4) and belongs on the ASKS file."
- **Settles it.** An MS Project run on a two-task file: open the verifier's `snlt_h1.xml` / `fnlt_h1.xml` with "Tasks
  will always honor their constraint dates" ON, recalculate, save as XML and read UID 3 Start / Finish / TotalSlack, UID
  4 Finish and the project finish (the claim expects 06-10 08:00 / 06-11 17:00 / C 06-12 17:00). **ASK-12.**

#### A0923-IMP-008 · IMP · ARTIFACT-GATED (ASK-13) — a percent lag through the vendored MPXJ writer

- **Claim (as sent).** The vendored MPXJ 16.2.0 writer writes a 25 % lag as `<LinkLag>25</LinkLag><LagFormat>19</LagFormat>`
  (MPXJ reads it back as 25.0 %); the importer reads LagFormat 19 / 20 LinkLag as TENTHS of a percent → a 60-minute lag
  for 600 (successor early start Mon 2026-06-08 09:00 for the hand-computed Tue 2026-06-09 10:00).
- **Mechanism reproduced.** Verifier P5 (`A0923-IMP-008.json`): **REPRODUCED-MECHANISM (artifact-gated)**; lead re-run:
  "FAIL S_pct25: model lag_minutes=60 (expected 600; predecessor duration 2400 min)", with the day and hour lags PASS,
  exit 1; control (LinkLag=250) "lag_minutes=600 (expected 600)", exit 0.
- **Why it is gated (verifier, verbatim, clipped).** "Whether a REAL MS Project FS+25% lag reaches MPXJ as 25 % is not
  established: MPXJ's MPP reader takes the raw integer stored in the .mpp as whole percent … so if MS Project stores
  tenths of a percent in the .mpp, MPXJ would hold 250 % and the importer would land on 25 % by two compensating errors.
  The corpus cannot settle this: 0 of 21,609 links in the 29 tracked .mpp and 0 in every committed MS-Project-derived
  MSPDI carry LagFormat 19/20."
- **Tier opinion (verifier).** "T1 (data-gated) … Zero instances in the committed corpus; percent lags are uncommon in
  construction schedules."
- **Settles it.** One operator .mpp with a percent lag + MS Project's own XML export of it (verifier: task P = 5d from
  Mon 2026-06-01, successors FS+25% and FS+25e%; save as .mpp AND Save As XML; note MS Project's displayed Start —
  hand expectation Tue 2026-06-09 10:00; the XML's `<LinkLag>` 25 vs 250 settles ADR-0026's premise for native MSPDI,
  `parse_mpp` on the .mpp settles the product's .mpp path). **ASK-13.**

#### A0923-IMP-009 · IMP · ARTIFACT-GATED (ASK-14) — LevelingDelayFormat is never read

- **Claim (as sent).** A task delay stored in working-day format 7 (`<LevelingDelay>9600</LevelingDelay>`, 9600 tenths =
  2 working days) is applied as 960 CLOCK minutes: start Tue 2026-03-03 08:00 for Wed 2026-03-04 08:00.
- **Mechanism reproduced.** Verifier P5 (`A0923-IMP-009.json`): **REPRODUCED-MECHANISM (artifact-gated)** — "no importer
  reads LevelingDelayFormat, so a TASK delay stored in format 7 ('d') is converted with // 10 to minutes and the engine
  adds those minutes as CLOCK time (engine/cpm.py:2565-2569 …)"; lead re-run: "LevelingDelay 9600 tenths, format 7:
  model leveling_delay_minutes = 960 / start wall 2026-03-03 08:00:00 finish wall 2026-03-03 17:00:00 expected
  2026-03-04 08:00:00 / 2026-03-04 17:00:00", AssertionError "delay applied on the wrong time base", exit 1; control
  (28800 tenths, format 8) PASS. (The first control command named a doubled directory and did not run — EXIT=2; the
  lead's second control file records the PASS.)
- **Why it is gated (verifier, verbatim, clipped).** "(1) the expectation rests on applying the DurationFormat note's
  working-day definition to LevelingDelayFormat by analogy … (2) the same Microsoft pages' own example writes a 3-day
  task delay as <LevelingDelay>3</LevelingDelay><LevelingDelayFormat>7</LevelingDelayFormat>, inconsistent with the
  tenths-of-a-minute encoding … (3) zero real instances - every task delay in the corpus is format 8."
- **Corpus.** Every task delay is format 8 and every booking delay format 7 (the class statement, reproduced by the
  verifier). The facts sheet carries "389 task delays all format 8, 75 booking delays all format 7"; the verifier
  counts 276 / 276 task delays over the 29 .mpp conversions and 173 / 173 over the committed MS-Project-derived XML
  ("The finder's figure '389 of 389' is NOT reproduced as a number") — **the figure 389 is UNVERIFIED**; the booking
  count 75 = 51 (.mpp conversions) + 24 (committed goldens) agrees with the verifier.
- **Tier opinion (verifier).** "T1 (data-gated), leaning low-exposure."
- **Settles it.** An MS Project save with a "2d" task Leveling Delay, exported as XML (verifier: a 1d task T7 from Mon
  2026-03-02 with Leveling Delay "2d", a twin T8 with "2ed", and a 1d task from Wed 2026-03-04 with "1d"; save as .mpp
  AND Save As XML; the XML shows whether format 7 is kept, MS Project's displayed Start / Finish settles the time base).
  **ASK-14.**

### Withdrawn after the falsification pass (1) — measured true, but a documented deliberate decision (charter §4)

**A0923-TST-003 — WITHDRAWN as a class (session 2, LEAD-DECISIONS-2; omitted from the register count).** The non-registration of `.claude/hooks/qc_session_start.sh` is a documented deliberate decision: `.claude/agents/README.md:40-41` "registering it must be done by a human — the assistant is deliberately barred from editing its own startup/hook config"; ADR-0344:84-86 "the `qc_session_start.sh` hook is still unregistered and still needs a human" (LEAD-DECISIONS-2 attributes that sentence to `.claude/skills/README.md:47-49`; the tree has it at docs/adr/0344:84-86 — recorded in `$S/wave5/drafter1b-disputes.txt` D-1, not changed silently); and CLAUDE.md:366-367 reads as an availability statement — so under charter §4 ("disagreement with a documented deliberate decision" is not an error) it is NOT a finding. **What survives is ONE sentence**, `.claude/skills/README.md:45` ("runs the gate *autonomously* on a throttle"), which describes a run no committed configuration performs: recorded as a doc-precision note inside U18's scope, **not counted, no reproducer** (`test_a0923_tst_003` REMOVED from `tests/audit/test_audit_20260923_tst.py`). ASK-04 (register the hook or reword CLAUDE.md:366-367) is WITHDRAWN — the decision is documented; there is nothing to ask. The refuter's verdict was NARROWED (packet R09, `A0923-TST-003.json`); its narrowed claim, verbatim: "At 8c71c639 .claude/skills/README.md:45 states in the present tense that `.claude/agents/qc-checker.md` 'runs the gate *autonomously* on a throttle', while no committed configuration runs it on any throttle (settings.json's two SessionStart matchers run only session_start.sh; qc_session_start.sh has never been registered in any commit) and that sentence does not say registration is pending — a doc-precision (T6) drift, not an instrument defect, because the non-registration itself is a documented deliberate decision awaiting a human (agents/README.md:39-41, ADR-0344:84-86) and CLAUDE.md:367 only names the trigger as one available route."

Refuter packet R09's eight attacks on it (session 2), quoted as for the retained blocks:

- **Falsification pass (session 2, refuter packet R09, `A0923-TST-003.json`)** — verdict **NARROWED**; at f1b691f3: **STILL-PRESENT**; confidence high.
  - A1 authority re-read: .claude/skills/README.md:45-46 …: '- **`.claude/agents/qc-checker.md`** runs the gate *autonomously* on a throttle and fixes what it finds. …' CLAUDE.md:366-367: '- The `qc-checker` subagent (`.claude/agents/`) runs the full gate, triages real errors vs environment-gated skips / flakes, and fixes them — on demand or via a throttled SessionStart trigger.' The committed config, .claude/settings.json, 'hooks.SessionStart' = two matchers (startup, resume), each with the single command 'bash .claude/hooks/session_start.sh'. … Reading: CLAUDE.md:367 'on demand or via a throttled SessionStart trigger' lists routes by which the agent CAN run — an availability statement … skills/README.md:45 is present-tense ('runs … on a throttle'), and in this repo 'throttle' is the SessionStart hook's own term
  - A2 deliberate-decision search: DELIBERATE — a direct hit on the settings.json half of the claim. ADR-0344:80-86 '### Deliberately NOT done … **`.claude/settings.json` untouched.** Skills need no registration, and the assistant is barred from editing its own startup/hook config (`.claude/agents/README.md`); the `qc_session_start.sh` hook is still unregistered and still needs a human.' .claude/agents/README.md:40-41 …: 'The script is committed, but **registering it must be done by a human** — the assistant is deliberately barred from editing its own startup/hook config. …' So 'settings.json never references qc_session_start.sh' is the designed, disclosed state pending a human action — under AGENT-RULES §7 not a finding. No ADR/HELD row addresses the skills/README sentence itself.
  - A3 independent method: (1) `python3 -c json.load(.claude/settings.json)` → SessionStart commands ['bash .claude/hooks/session_start.sh', 'bash .claude/hooks/session_start.sh'], qc_session_start registered: False; (2) `git log -S'qc_session_start' -- .claude/settings.json` → empty: never registered in any commit since the hook landed (ed7af32b 2026-06-19, #167); … (5) run in a throwaway `git clone --shared`: `bash .claude/hooks/qc_session_start.sh` prints the '[qc-checker] Scheduled quality-control is due' directive, exit 0, writes .git/qc-checker-last-run, and a second call within the interval prints 0 bytes — the instrument works; registration is the only gap.
  - A4 environment: none of this depends on TZ, Python or library versions. One caveat cuts the other way: a human could have registered the hook in `.claude/settings.local.json` or `~/.claude/settings.json` on a workstation, invisibly to the repo — so 'never wired' is provable only for the committed configuration.
  - A5 measures the stated thing / population recount: the reproducer checks phrase presence and the SessionStart command list — it measures the committed wiring and the sentence, which is the whole claim. … Population: 2 SessionStart matchers × 1 command each; 1 hook script unregistered; 4 tracked references to it, all prose. Practical difference: small — CLAUDE.md:181, full-gate, steward:96 and session-close all require the lead to run the gate before every commit/push regardless of the agent
  - A6 alternative witness: agents/README.md:57-58 'Once registered, every new/resumed session checks the throttle and, when due, asks the assistant to run the `qc-checker` sweep' — the SAME repo file that skills/README.md:45 names states the conditional correctly ('Once registered'). And the sentence at skills/README.md:45 was written in commit d57e230f (#527, 2026-08-03) — the ADR-0344 commit whose own text says the hook 'is still unregistered'.
  - A7 current main (f1b691f3): CURRENT MAIN f1b691f3: `git log 8c71c639..HEAD -- .claude/settings.json .claude/skills/README.md CLAUDE.md .claude/agents/README.md .claude/hooks/` empty; settings.json SessionStart commands unchanged (qc_session_start registered: False); skills/README.md:45 and CLAUDE.md:367 byte-identical. STILL-PRESENT (as narrowed).
  - A8 steelman, and why it fails: STEELMAN: 'The hook is committed and throttled by design; registration is a human's job and every relevant document says so …; CLAUDE.md:367 merely lists the trigger as an available route. Nothing here is an instrument that cannot fail — the gate the lead must run is unaffected.' This largely holds: it disposes of the settings.json half and of CLAUDE.md, and it lowers the surviving sentence to doc precision. It does not fully hold for skills/README.md:45 itself: the sentence is unconditional present tense, 'throttle' is the hook's term
  - Refuter's tier opinion: T6 (organizational / doc precision) for the surviving sentence at skills/README.md:45 — one clause ('once the hook is registered, see agents/README') fixes it. NOT T5: the unregistered hook is a documented 'Deliberately NOT done' pending a human, the hook script works when invoked, and no gate depends on it. The packet's T5/TST framing over-states it.

## WP plans as executed — load-bearing assumptions and how each was tested

**Deviation, stated plainly.** Charter §9 requires that "before each WP's first probe, write the WP plan into
the ledger and attack it (QC-3): list its load-bearing assumptions (population, oracle, witness, mechanism)
and test each on the pristine tree." **That was not done first this session.** No WP plan was written into a
ledger before its first probe; the QC-3 attack was instead **folded into the finder / verifier protocol** —
every finder recorded a control, a class census and its own refutation attempts per claim (§6.1), and every
verifier ran the five mandatory refutation attempts per claim (§6.2). The plans below are **reconstructed after
the fact** from those records. What the fold costs: a per-claim attack cannot catch a wrong population or a
missing oracle for a work package as a whole — the clearest case is WP-CPM, whose oracle (the 44-file
stored-value corpus) was never rebuilt, so its differential census never ran. Next sessions should write each
WP plan, with its attacked assumptions, into the ledger before the first probe.

### WP0 — baseline, environment, census, gap map (lead + Scout A)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The charter describes this repository | the §0 commands at the session start | PASSED — main moved by exactly one commit (#714) |
| The installed instruments are the ones CI uses | the ruff on PATH checked against pyproject's `>=0.16.1,<0.17`; the NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT install line re-measured | stale ruff 0.15.11 on PATH → every lint ran as `python3 -m ruff` (0.16.8); `/usr/local/bin/python3` absent → installed with `/usr/bin/python3.11` |
| Pristine main is green | full suite + CI-scoped parity at the base | 1 failure (→ DUPLICATE-OF R-32, new local recipe), 5 skips triaged; parity 249 passed |
| The file population is `git ls-files` at the base | `census/classify.py` over `git ls-files -z` | 2,226 files (698 intake) |
| The route population comes from the running app, not a list | routes enumerated from `create_app(SessionState())` | 156 routes |
| The 44-file stored-value corpus (22,105 activities at ADR-0523) is the CPM oracle | **not tested** — the corpus was not rebuilt (JVM lock contention and budget) | carried to WP-CPM as its first step |

### WP1-INH — inherited claims (Scout A, Scout C)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The prior ledgers' open rows are the INH population | Scout A read in full `AUDIT-2026-06-25`, `-07-13`, `-07-14`, `-08-13` (+ plan), `-08-16`, `-08-27`, every `audit/*.md`, `docs/risks.md` and `OPERATOR-REQUESTS.md` (its reading notes mark each "READ IN FULL") | 300 rows |
| A row closed later is named by the closing ADR or ledger | a grep per row id; Scout A's caveat: "an ADR can close a row without naming it" (`AUDIT-2026-08-16.md:416-418`) | 179 closed · 59 still open by grep · 62 unknown |
| Carried-forward items outside the R-register are findable | Scout C read HANDOFF's current section, HANDOFF-ARCHIVE back to 2026-09-01 (62 sections, archive lines 4–3521; it stopped at line 3522), NEXT-SESSION-PROMPT in full and the not-done / residual sections of ADR-0472..0526; each item grepped against the 80 R-rows | 158 unique items, 100 unregistered; the archive before 2026-09-01 was **not harvested** |
| Each inherited row is re-provable | **not tested** — per-row re-proof not done (tabulated only) | carried to WP-INH |

### DOC / TST — documentation and instrument truth (Finder DOC → packets P06–P11)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The population is every checkable present-tense claim in the doc family, skills and agents | Finder DOC extracted claims file by file, each with the command that checks it (`claims.tsv`) | 468 claims (364 TRUE / 82 FALSE / 22 not checkable here) in 26 defect classes |
| The oracle is the tree and the running app at the base | each claim's command run at `8c71c639` (in-process TestClient for routes, `git ls-files` for tracked files, the engine for figures) | 82 FALSE → 26 classes → 16 DOC + 10 TST |
| A FALSE statement is present-tense, not a historical record (rule 7) | each verifier checked dated sections and commit history | several NARROWED (e.g. DOC-004: 2 of 9 statements historical, 1 not asserting absence; DOC-015: PARITY-REPORT's "374" was exact when written) |
| The finder's check measures the stated thing | fresh verifiers built their own checks (P09: server + real Chromium; P10/P11: shadow trees and scratch repos) | 26 of 26 finder-DOC claims REPRODUCED; a DOC verifier surfaced one new candidate (WEB-001), verified in P09 |

### IMP — importers (Finder F1 → packet P03; IMP-005 from F3 → P05)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The mechanisms are the ones Scout C items 3–6 and Scout A lead SA-L07 describe (xlsx `r=`, single-block calendar, exception hours, XER `TASK.clndr_id`, declared encoding) | five probes with inline inputs, each with a control (e.g. the same hours as two touching blocks) | all five mechanisms reproduced; the UTF-16 and all-`r`-less sub-claims REFUTED as silent (they fail loudly) |
| The oracles are independent of the importer | public specifications retrieved 2026-09-23 (MS-OI29500 §18.3.1.4; MS Project XML schema; Oracle P6 help; W3C XML 1.0 §4.3.3) + hand arithmetic; verifier third-party witnesses: MPXJ 16.2.0 under the JVM lock, openpyxl, LibreOffice | the witnesses agreed with the specification's value for H1–H4; for H5 the reference library MPXJ behaves like the engine by default (the W3C rule, not MPXJ, is the oracle) |
| The committed corpus shows the reach | census over the 42 tracked MSPDI documents (11 `.gz`), MPXJ conversions of the 29 committed `.mpp` (under the lock), 98 xlsx packages | H2 / H3 / H4 / H5 latent (0 committed files); committed Acumen exports use optional `r=` (149 of 156 rows of one sheet), but only 5 rows have the literal first-cell-only shape and none starts at column B (verifier) |
| A documented decision does not already hold each item | ADR search per hypothesis | ADR-0497 and ADR-0028 premises falsified (IMP-002, IMP-003); exception hours HELD-BY ADR-0503; ADR-0526:57's scope note does not cover IMP-001's consequence |

### CPM / MET — engine and metrics (Finder F2 → packet P04)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| MS Project's stored values in committed goldens and Acumen's recorded figures are the oracles | read from the goldens (e.g. EVM2 `FinishDate` 2012-10-04) and ADR-0108 | used for H1 / H3; SSI's 24 intake Directional-Path workbooks for H4 |
| A mechanism is causal, not correlated | in-memory patches as controls, no file modified (e.g. lifting the `cpm.py:903` gate gives EVM2 2012-10-04 / NFI −22 exactly, 28 other files unmoved) | H1 causal (then HELD); H4's floor inert (REFUTED) |
| The population is the committed schedules | 29 MSPDI files (15 goldens incl. 11 `.gz` + 14 intake `.xml`) | the 29 intake `.mpp` were **not converted** (JVM lock held by a parity run) |
| The hypothesis's premise is the authority's (H2: CLAUDE.md requires stored slack in the grid) | read CLAUDE.md:219-221's scope | NOT-A-FINDING as posed; narrowed to MET-002 |
| The margin fit is on one basis (07-14 NEW-2) | three- and two-version in-memory fixtures + hand arithmetic; the verifier rebuilt its own fixture | MET-001 reproduced on both fixtures (different figures, same class) |

### AI / CUI — AI layer and Law-1 guards (Finder F3 → packets P01, P02, P05)

| load-bearing assumption | how it was tested | result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The oracles are the repo's contracts, independent of the gate code | CLAUDE.md's figure-gate description; ADR-0131, 0132, 0145, 0239, 0396, 0402, 0404, 0469, 0070; RFC 6761 §6.3 (retrieved 2026-09-23) — every quote re-read verbatim by the verifiers | held |
| Nothing leaves the container while probing | scripted fake backends through the injectable openers; a kill switch (AF_INET/AF_INET6 connect refused, non-loopback names refused); verifiers under `unshare -n` | the kill switch's BLOCKED lists stayed empty in the gateway probes; no AI endpoint, gateway or DNS server was contacted |
| The check measures the served output, not a helper | verifiers drove `/api/ask/{name}`, `/api/ask`, `/api/ai/narrative`, `/api/ai/briefing`, `/api/translate`, `/settings`, `/launch`, and rendered in Chromium; CUI-001 re-measured with a real resolver in an isolated network namespace, no Python patching | all 12 F3 claims REPRODUCED — on the served route where the claim is about a route, on the real transport for the redirect and name-resolution claims |
| The populations are censused, not sampled | tokenizer consumers; 1,131 of 1,791 numeric code points (Python 3.11.15 / UCD 14.0.0); 28 loaded terms × suffix forms; every `build_opener` site; 32 absolute-locality doc lines | recorded per block |
| The result is not environment-gated | TZ=UTC vs America/New_York; Python 3.11 at route level (the verifiers' 3.13 lacked pydantic), then the reproducers on the wave-3 3.13 venv | identical everywhere measured; Windows name resolution UNVERIFIED (ASK-01) |

### WP-CPM (session 5) — written and attacked BEFORE its finders (with one recorded deviation)

Session 5 wrote the WP-CPM plan down (a session record) and attacked its eight load-bearing assumptions on the pristine tree
before any finder was spawned — the QC-3 step sessions 1–2 folded into the finder protocol. The plan and its attack
table are inserted verbatim in the "Session 5" section at the top of this ledger ("WP-CPM plan and its QC-3 attack").
Recorded deviation: the corpus rebuild and the first pass of the differential census ran about 30 minutes before the
plan was written. What fell: **P7** ("a date difference on the census is a defect") as a blanket premise — every
non-exact class was mapped to its register row or ADR (finder F-RES) before any could become a CANDIDATE.
